A two-target regression proves only the destination target is damaged (`engine.ts` `performDash`/landing branch; `public/99lc/game-config.json` `clawDash.tuning`).

### M110. Authored critical attacks do not use the available world-space CRIT cue
- **Expected:** Spear tip criticals, deep Claw strike, Knife-spider impale, Katana overhead/execute and Sword opening visibly distinguish their critical result.
- **Actual before the fix:** `criticalHitMs` was set only by the Spear-spin head zone; the remaining critical multipliers changed health silently despite the renderer already supporting `CRIT` (`engine.ts` interim `damageEnemy`/`renderEnemy`).
- **Failure scenario:** consume a Sword opening or land the Claw deep strike → damage increases, but the target shows no critical feedback.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-16 — `damageEnemy` marks every authored sweet spot/critical behavior with the shared 650 ms cue (externally overridable), while the existing Spear-spin headshot retains its own tuning. A table regression covers all named critical families (`engine.ts` `damageEnemy`/`renderEnemy`; focused engine tests).

## Sim sweep triage (2026-07-17)

> **Scope:** triage of the errors in sweep `DataBase/Simulations/sweep-20260716-230144` (100k requested games per AI level): four Int32-overflow game crashes (ai-0 #3120; ai-2 #2382/#2605/#2974), one unhandled NRE that killed the entire ai-1 arm after batch 3, and three non-fatal `CRIT … didn't do anything` bot stalls in the ai-1 log.

### M111. TickAsync's catch re-indexed the live GamesList and killed the process (M51's open race, now fatal)
- **Expected:** a per-game exception in the readiness loop is logged/sinked and the loop continues; concurrent `GamesList` mutation must never crash the host.
- **Actual:** `Global.GamesList` is a plain `List<GameClass>`; the sim runner adds thousands of games (`BotGameFactory`) and the watchdog removes stuck ones (`SimulationRunner`) while the timer thread iterates the same list in `CheckIfReady.TickAsync`. A racing `Add`/`Remove` exposes a transient null slot: `games[i]` returned null (NRE inside the loop body, caught) — and the catch block itself then re-indexed `games[i]`, throwing a second NRE that escaped the catch, propagated through the `async void` timer handler and terminated the process. The ai-1 sweep arm lost batches 4–19. This is exactly the "Still open" residual recorded under M51; the same race exists in production (web/Discord threads add games while the timer ticks).
- **Failure scenario:** bulk sim creates games / watchdog removes a stuck one while the timer thread is mid-iteration → null slot read → catch re-indexes the mutated list → unhandled NRE → process dies, taking every live game with it.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-17 — every `GamesList` mutation now locks the list instance (adds: `WebGameService`, `BotGameFactory`, `General`, `AdminPanel`; removes: `CheckIfReady.HandleLastRound`, all four `SimulationRunner` sites); `TickAsync` iterates a snapshot taken under the same lock, null-guards each entry, and its catch uses the captured `game` reference instead of re-indexing. `HandlePredic2`'s `GamesList.Find` + `game!` (the earlier member of this race family named in M51) got a null-guard. Unsynchronized *reads* on request threads remain (e.g. `Global.GetGame`) — an exception there stays inside the request and cannot kill the process.

### M112. Battleship's live rules diverged from the approved designer review
- **Expected:** the 2026-07-17 choices in `BATTLESHIP-DESIGNER-REVIEW.md` are one authoritative runtime contract: one global/idempotent Boarding transition, dead-deck-only reset rules, source/deck-aware weapons, compulsory CAPTURE, non-predictive defeat, deterministic first-player tie resolution, physical-board movement effects, legal bot knowledge/actions, reviewed UI visibility and exact GDD assets. Explicit rejects I02/I16/D14 and the own-board-only Greek Fire exception remain exceptions.
- **Actual before the fix:** Boarding could retrigger and resolve only one side; repeated dead-deck shots reset turns; weapon selection lost source/module identity; CAPTURE could toggle or softlock and was not represented end-to-end; poison/reveal/summon movement crossed incorrect boundaries; defeat predicted ammo sufficiency; setup and bot paths bypassed player rules; legal weapon/status/armor UI state was incomplete; Discus and dead model scaffolding appeared live despite no mechanic.
- **Failure scenario:** destroy the first Mid, fire into an already dead deck, capture a Close ship, or destroy one of several identical weapon modules → bonuses/conversions repeat, the shot series continues illegally, the owner cannot complete the forced own-board action, or the wrong module remains selectable; clients and bots then act on state that the server should have hidden or rejected.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-17 — implemented D01–D18, I01/I03–I15/I17–I20, Part D and the approved Part E defaults in the Battleship engine/service/hub/Vue flow. I02/I16/D14 remain rejected as recorded. The exact nine GDD bitmap assets ship under `Web/VueClient/public/battleship/icons/`; `BattleshipGDD.md`, `WEB-BACKEND.md`, `WEB-CLIENT.md` and the review implementation record now describe the live contract. Core anchors: `BattleshipGameEngine.GetUsableWeapons`/`TriggerBoarding`/`MoveSummons`/`CheckWinCondition`; `BattleshipService.SelectWeapon`/`PassBoardingTurn`/`MapPlayer`; `CombatPhase.vue`, `WeaponBar.vue`, `CellComponent.vue` and `store/battleship.ts`.

### M113. Battleship follow-up state projection loses summon/reveal identity and serializes the bot's whole reset chain
- **Expected:** the 2026-07-17 follow-up specification is authoritative for Battleship presentation and edge cases: dead summons disappear from active status; historical trail cells retain every visiting summon type; Scout and post-destruction reveals distinguish empty water from intact ships; a shot into any already-dead deck is a miss; Maneuvering Double reconciles its stale hit after sinking; collision damage is identical in both views; only a regular Ram may turn back; CAPTURE/Devastated and duplicate ship identities stay unambiguous; bot reset shots have the same two-second interaction window as a human; projectile/impact/sinking VFX never change resolution.
- **Actual before the fix:** `Cell.SummonTrail`/`CellDto.SummonTrail` are one boolean and the Vue fallback converts every unknown trail to `Ram`; the local trail cache can recover a type only while that summon remains in the current DTO. Reveal helpers set only `IsRevealed`, so empty Scout/blast cells have no miss mark, and `RevealShip` explicitly skips occupied neighbours. Maneuvered historical hit flags are never reconciled on final death. Ram scratches do not persist the same hit identity in fog. Every summon can enter `WaitingForTurnBack`. `ProcessBotTurn` resolves up to 100 reset shots synchronously while the game lock is held. `CellComponent` gives destroyed red priority over Devastated and uses purple for intact revealed occupancy; projectile VFX always start at the target board's bottom centre as one cannonball (`BattleshipModels.Cell`; `BattleshipGameEngine.RevealShip`/`RevealArea`/`MoveSummons`/`MarkRamDamageOnBoard`; `BattleshipService.ProcessBotTurn`; `CombatPhase.vue` trail fallback; `CellComponent.vue`; `useVfx.ts`).
- **Failure scenario:** send a Scout through a Ram trail and let Drakkar freeze it, then sink a moved Maneuvering Double beside another ship while the bot has a reset chain → both trail legends say Ram, empty revealed cells have no permanent miss glyph, the adjacent intact ship stays dark, the old destroyed-deck coordinate remains red, the two views may disagree on the rammed Triple deck, and the bot completes every follow-up shot before the human can deploy a summon.
- **Fix direction:** make trails a persistent set of summon types per physical cell; centralize reveal-result marking and reveal ownership; reconcile Maneuver history on sink; reject dead-deck damage before hit flags/logs; restrict re-entry to regular Ram; serialize one bot action per delayed hub pump; project typed shot source/projectile data for privacy-safe VFX; update the Battleship GDD/backend/client contracts and add focused regression coverage.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-17 — `Cell.SummonTrails` is now a persistent typed set projected to both boards; active DTOs contain only living summons. `RevealCell` owns empty-miss/anonymous-occupancy projection and credits only its actor, while `RevealShip` exposes occupied Space and reconciles Maneuvering Double's vacated destroyed deck on final death. Dead-deck shots return miss before hit/Mast/reset logic; Ram collision marks the exact deck in both views; only regular Ram can re-enter and spawn/re-entry runs collision hazards immediately. CAPTURE, black Devastated cells and duplicate fleet names remain unambiguous. The bot advances through a two-second one-action hub pump with the lock released. Typed shot source/target/projectile data drives privacy-safe arrow/stone/buckshot/fire flight plus splash, splinters and sinking VFX. (`BattleshipModels.Cell`/`ShotResult`; `BattleshipGameEngine.RevealCell`/`RevealShip`/`MoveSummons`; `BattleshipService.ProcessBotStep`; `GameHub.RunBattleshipBotPump`; `ProjectileLayer.vue`; `CellComponent.vue`.)

### M114. Джон Сноу does not start in Черный Замок, so opening loyalty pays when it should be blocked
- **Expected:** whenever Джон Сноу is in the roster, initial placement puts him at exact place 4, immediately starts the three-action-round Черный Замок hold and leaves him on neither loyalty side for rounds 1–3 unless an explicit position effect moves him.
- **Actual:** `HandleEventsBeforeFirstRound` calls `JonSnow.FinalizeInitialPositions` before the common callers assign `Status.PlaceAtLeaderBoard`. `FinalizeInitialPositions` neither moves the list nor recognizes the default place value as 4, so the initial hold never starts and Jon keeps his random seat (`CharacterPassives.cs:487`; `JonSnow.cs:426-449`; callers assign places only afterward in `General.cs:293-296`, `WebGameService.cs:400-405`, `BotGameFactory.cs:80-83` and draft completion).
- **Failure scenario:** Jon rolls into place 1–3 or 5–6 → round-1 allies win fights → Черный Замок awards bonus points even though the intended opening three turns should place Jon at neutral place 4.
- **Fix direction:** make the final initialization step move Jon to list index 3 and arm the initial hold before the shared caller assigns numeric places.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-17 — `JonSnow.FinalizeInitialPositions` now receives the mutable authoritative list, moves Jon to index 3 after every other opening placement effect, and arms the hold through action rounds 1–3 before the common callers assign place numbers. Place 4 remains neutral in `AwardBlackCastleLoyalty`, so those opening rounds cannot award side-victory points unless a later explicit mover removes Jon and `FinalizePositionEffects` cancels the hold.

### m49. Score-derived achievement progress overflowed Int32 and crashed HandleLastRound
- **Expected:** `TrackGameEnd` measures achievement progress for any legal final score.
- **Actual:** Гордон Фримен's Halflife 3 super multiplier `P × P^P` legitimately produces round-10 settlements above `int.MaxValue` (P=9 → ≈3.5 billion; score is decimal by design). `(int)Math.Floor(...)` on `RoundTenRegularPoints` (`g_quad_damage`) threw `OverflowException`, aborting `HandleLastRound` — the game errored at round 11 and was additionally reported stuck. The same unguarded cast sat on Итачи's `TotalStolenPoints` (`c_itachi_tax` — Цукуеми inherits the complete transformed Halflife settlement) and Наруто's `ShadowPointsTransferred` (`c_naruto_rasengan`). Four games crashed in the sweep; every lineup contained Гордон Фримен.
- **Failure scenario:** Gordon attempts the Halflife 3 release on round 10 with ≥9 pending regular points → settlement > 2³¹ → `TrackGameEnd` throws at game end → no winner/rewards, game stuck at round 11.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-17 — added `ToProgress(decimal)` (floor + clamp to [0, int.MaxValue]) in `AchievementClass` and routed all three casts through it. Progress semantics unchanged for every legitimate sub-int value.
- **Later rework:** 2026-07-21 — Halflife 3 settlement was changed from the historical `P × P^P` described above to exactly `P^P`. The guarded progress conversion remains required because legal powers can still exceed `int.MaxValue`.

### m50. L1 "must attack" bots could end their turn with no action after one rejected blind retry
- **Expected:** a bot whose policy forbids Block (Наруто clones, Спарта/Aggress carriers, blades/Мишень/event plans) always ends readiness with an attack, or a legal Block/Skip when nothing is attackable.
- **Actual:** in the legacy (L1) attack path, when the preference-roll attack was rejected, the `isBlock == noBlock` recovery branch picked **one** random target and ignored `AttackPlayer`'s return value. A second rejection (sealed Мадара is still present in the legacy target pool at rounds 9–10; pet-pair/Вампур re-attack rules likewise reject) left the bot with no attack, no Block, no Skip — tripping the readiness CRIT guard `didn't do anything and auto move didn't as well`, which force-assigned Block/Skip. Three occurrences in the ai-1 sweep log (all Наруто, rounds 9/10). Same family as M66 (Gordon, fair path).
- **Failure scenario:** L1 Наруто clone at round 9 rolls sealed Мадара → rejected; the blind retry randomly picks Мадара again → silently rejected → no action → the CRIT fallback forces Skip, wasting the clone's turn (clones are designed to always attack).
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-17 — the recovery branch now retries the whole remaining pool (mirroring `HandleBotAttackRandom`'s loop) and, if every candidate is rejected, falls through to the authoritative `HandleAttack(-10)` fallback — Наруто clones route through `AttackOrSkipInsteadOfBlock` (attack anyone legal, else a real Skip), everyone else gets a legal Block (`BotsBehavior.cs` legacy `isBlock == noBlock` branch; `GameReactions.cs` `AttackOrSkipInsteadOfBlock`).

### m51. Король Сервера appears at the bottom instead of replacing Тупой бастард in the passive panel
- **Expected:** reaching 228 Skill replaces the visible Тупой бастард card in place with Король Сервера, preserving that slot in Discord/web passive presentation.
- **Actual:** `JonSnow.TryBecomeKing` removes Тупой бастард from the passive list and merely reveals the already-existing Король Сервера entry near the bottom of `characters.json`; order-preserving clients therefore render the replacement at that lower index (`JonSnow.cs:79-87`; `SkillsPanel.vue:140-163`).
- **Failure scenario:** expand Тупой бастард, reach 228 Skill → its card disappears and the newly visible Король Сервера description appears several rows lower rather than in the vacated slot.
- **Fix direction:** move the existing hidden Король Сервера `Passive` object into the exact former Тупой бастард index while revealing it.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-17 — `JonSnow.TryBecomeKing` now removes the existing hidden King object from its old position, reveals it and assigns that same object into Тупой бастард's exact list index. Both Discord's ordered fields and Vue's index-keyed expanded card therefore retain the replacement in place.

### m52. Черный Замок has no public marker on place 4
- **Expected:** while Джон Сноу's Черный Замок mechanic exists, the fixed fourth leaderboard cell is visibly marked as the castle in Discord, web and replay projections.
- **Actual:** `GameUpdateMess.CustomLeaderBoardBeforeNumber` adds public Джон `🐺` marks but never annotates the fixed place-4 cell; web/replay use the same projection (`GameUpdateMess.cs:245-334`; `WebGameService.cs:196-222`, `PopulateCustomLeaderboard`).
- **Failure scenario:** open any Джон match → the leaderboard gives no visual indication which cell is Черный Замок or where the upper/lower-side boundary lies.
- **Fix direction:** add a public `🏰` prefix to exact place 4 through the shared custom-leaderboard projection whenever the roster contains the Черный Замок holder.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-17 — `GameUpdateMess.CustomLeaderBoardBeforeNumber` now emits `🏰` for exact place 4 whenever Джон Сноу's roster entry still carries Черный Замок. Discord uses it directly; web and replay receive the same prefix through `WebGameService.PopulateCustomLeaderboard`.
- **Reversed:** 2026-07-21 (M133) — the `🏰` (and the two `🐺` weakest-player marks) are no longer public: both emitters are now gated on `player1.GetPlayerId() == jonSnow.GetPlayerId()`, so only the Джон Сноу player sees the castle cell and wolves. The Castle mechanic (place-4 hold, side-victory loyalty) is unchanged; only the marker's visibility is now owner-only.

### M115. 99LC «Включить функции DualSense» could never open the WebHID chooser — no production transport was wired
- **Expected:** in a secure Chromium context the explicit `Enable DualSense features` action opens the browser HID chooser for a DualSense/DualSense Edge pad and, once granted, raises feedback to Tier 2 (adaptive triggers + rumble); denial or unsupported contexts fall back without affecting input.
- **Actual:** the documented-as-intended verified-hardware boundary (WEB-CLIENT.md §Tier 2): both `DualSenseFeedbackController` constructions omitted the `outputs` argument (`engine.ts` constructor and `applyControlDefinition`), so `enableEnhancedFeatures()` returned false in `feedback.ts` before any WebHID code ran; the only `requestDevice` caller (`DualSenseWebHidDriver`) was never instantiated in production and no packet serializer existed in the repo. Designer verdict 2026-07-18: cross the boundary — ship the real transport, USB + Bluetooth, with physical verification performed by the designer.
- **Failure scenario:** connect a DualSense in Chrome on the hosted HTTPS page, select the DualSense scheme, click «Включить функции DualSense» → no permission prompt ever appears, the toast always reports enhanced feedback as not enabled and adaptive triggers can never activate.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-18 — new `dualsense-serializers.ts` ships the production transport: USB output report `0x02` and Bluetooth report `0x31` (sequence nibble, `0x10` tag, CRC-32 over `0xA2` + report id + payload) built from the shared 47-byte common payload (rumble motors plus per-hand adaptive-trigger blocks from each effect's `adaptiveProfile`; neutral writes full-off mode `0x05`). `createLastChancesDualSenseEnhancedOutput` binds `navigator.hid` behind the Sony allowlist and is injected as `outputs.enhanced` at both engine construction sites; unsupported/insecure contexts keep Tier 0/1 with a dedicated unsupported toast. Trigger-byte feel (mode/scaling) may still be tuned after the designer's physical Tier-2 pass; the `/99lc/dualsense-harness.html` page supports that iteration. The first pass also read calibration feature report `0x05` over Bluetooth — that regression is catalogued and fixed as M116.

### M116. M115's Bluetooth prepare step froze controller input — the pad was flipped into extended report mode
- **Expected:** enabling Tier 2 over Bluetooth leaves controller input untouched: the Gamepad API keeps reading the pad while WebHID sends output reports alongside it.
- **Actual:** the first M115 implementation read calibration feature report `0x05` in a Bluetooth `prepare` hook before the neutral packet. That read flips a BT DualSense from simple mode (standard-typed `0x01` input reports) into extended mode (vendor-typed `0x31` input reports), which Chromium's Gamepad API cannot map — controller input froze while HID output kept working, and the flip persists until the pad power-cycles. The flip is unnecessary for output: the pad accepts CRC-framed `0x31` output reports while staying in simple mode (verified on the designer's physical pad via a standalone trigger-lab page that never reads `0x05` and works).
- **Failure scenario:** BT DualSense → «Включить функции DualSense» → grant → mouse-driven game actions rumble and set trigger resistance, but the pad's buttons/sticks/triggers stop driving the game entirely until the controller is turned off and back on.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-18 (same change-set) — removed the `prepare` hook end-to-end (serializer interface member, driver call, `receiveFeatureReport` on the device shape, related tests); `BluetoothDualSenseSerializer` now carries a comment documenting why the report mode must never be touched.
- **Re-verified:** 2026-07-18 (later the same day) — the freeze was re-reported from a build that still contained the `0x05` read; a triage pass initially concluded (from SDL's PS5 driver, which claims any effects output packet flips the pad) that Bluetooth Tier 2 was impossible and briefly shipped a USB-only restriction, then reverted it based on a lab check believed to show input surviving Bluetooth output. **Correction (same day, M118):** that lab check was wrong — the designer's controlled retest showed the trigger-lab page loses Gamepad-API input the moment its first Bluetooth output packet is sent, i.e. SDL's claim holds for this pad/firmware. Any BT effects output flips the pad; the durable fix is the M118 WebHID input reader, not any output byte recipe.

### M117. Phase 11's canonical soldier migration leaves city rendering on deleted veteran/recovery collections
- **Expected:** opening a city derives wounded-veteran treatment and cohort recovery status from the canonical per-soldier army state; an army with no wounded or recovering soldiers renders normally.
- **Actual before the fix:** Phase 11 migrates legacy `army.veterans` and `army.recoveries` into `army.unitInstances`, then deletes both compatibility collections. `EmpiresEndgame.vue` still called `Object.entries(state.army.veterans)` whenever a Medical Academy was operational and `.filter` on `state.army.recoveries` for every visible cohort. The production epidemic QA fixture builds an Academy, so selecting its infected city threw `TypeError: Cannot convert undefined or null to object` and blanked the city projection; a city with a recruited cohort could hit the same stale-read family while building its recovery badge (`engine.ts` `normalizeArmyUnitInstances`; `pages/EmpiresEndgame.vue` pre-fix `cityViews`; `cypress/e2e/empires-endgame.cy.ts` epidemic scenario).
- **Failure scenario:** restore a schema-v14 campaign containing an operational Medical Academy or a recruited cohort, then open the city → the page can crash before epidemic, cohort or treatment details render, including when the corresponding canonical list is empty.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-18 — cohort recovery counts/ready cons now come from the cohort's canonical member instances, and the Medical Academy projection filters/sorts the same canonical dictionary by stable ID, exposes only `veteran && wounds > 0`, and passes that instance ID to `treatVeteran`. The full epidemic Cypress scenario exercises the Academy city with an empty veteran list, while the expedition browser flow carries a canonical recruited cohort; both render without an exception (`EmpiresEndgame.vue` `cityViews`; `engine.ts` `treatVeteran`; `cypress/e2e/empires-endgame.cy.ts`; `cypress/e2e/empires-endgame-expedition.cy.ts`).

### M118. Any Bluetooth effects output flips the DualSense into extended report mode — Tier 2 enable froze controller input
- **Expected:** enabling Tier 2 over Bluetooth keeps the pad fully playable: controller input keeps driving the game while WebHID delivers rumble and adaptive triggers.
- **Actual:** on the designer's pad/firmware, receiving *any* CRC-framed `0x31` effects output over Bluetooth — including the neutral packet written at enable — flips the pad from simple mode (standard `0x01` input reports) into extended mode (vendor-typed `0x31` input reports), exactly as SDL's PS5 driver warns. Chromium's Gamepad API cannot map the vendor-typed reports, so `navigator.getGamepads()` freezes everywhere (any tab) until the pad power-cycles. The M116 fix was correct but insufficient: it removed the `0x05` feature-report read, yet the first output packet triggers the same flip. Designer evidence 2026-07-18: after the in-game freeze, input was dead in every page until power-cycle, and the root `dualsense-trigger-lab.html` reproduces the freeze as soon as it connects and sends its first output — disproving the earlier lab-based re-verification recorded under M116 (`dualsense-serializers.ts` `BluetoothDualSenseSerializer`; `dualsense-hid.ts` `enableEnhancedFeatures`).
- **Failure scenario:** BT DualSense → «Включить функции DualSense» → grant the chooser → rumble/adaptive triggers work for mouse-driven actions, but the pad's sticks/buttons/triggers stop driving the game (and every other page) until the controller is turned off and back on.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-18 — the flip is accepted as pad firmware behavior and compensated on the input side: new `dualsense-input.ts` parses the extended `0x31` input reports (sticks, analog triggers, hat, all buttons) into canonical standard-gamepad order, and `DualSenseHidInputReader` exposes the latest reading as a synthetic standard-mapping pad with a 500 ms staleness cutoff. `DualSenseWebHidDriver` starts the reader on Bluetooth enable and surfaces it via `hidInputSnapshot()`; `DualSenseFeedbackController.hidGamepadSnapshot()` forwards it, and `engine.ts` `pollGamepad` prefers a fresh WebHID reading over the frozen Gamepad-API list so the frozen pad cannot win arbitration. Because the pad stays flipped until power-cycle, disabling enhanced output (and output write failures) over Bluetooth keeps the device open input-only with an honest `controls-only`/`error` capability message, and re-enable reuses the retained device without a second chooser prompt; page teardown closes fully. USB is untouched (Gamepad API keeps working there).

### M119. Tier-2 DualSense effects never stopped — one HID write left the motors energized until the next effect
- **Expected:** a Tier-2 rumble effect ends after its `durationMs`, like Tier 1's `playEffect({ duration })`; between cues the pad is silent and only the resting adaptive-trigger state remains.
- **Actual:** `DualSenseWebHidDriver.play()` wrote one output report per effect and returned; nothing ever wrote a motor-off packet afterward, so the motors stayed energized until the *next* effect or a full `neutralize()`. Combined with the long `ramp` (600 ms) / `tension` (520 ms) profiles emitted on every combo-node advance, this produced the reported "rumbles all the time" — over WebHID the rumble was literally continuous (`dualsense-hid.ts` `play`; `feedback.ts`).
- **Failure scenario:** enable DualSense features (USB or BT) → pull a trigger through one gate → the pad keeps buzzing indefinitely until another cue or disable.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-18, as part of the per-weapon haptics rework — every effect played on the enhanced output now schedules a stop at its `durationMs` that re-writes the **baseline** (motors off, each hand's resting/detent adaptive-trigger block kept): `buildDualSenseBaselinePayload` + `baseline()` on both serializers, driver `setBaseline`/`writeBaseline` with deep-equal dedup and restore-on-enable, `DualSenseFeedbackController.scheduleEnhancedStop` on an injectable scheduler (superseded by a newer effect, cancelled by neutralize/dispose/off). The same rework replaced continuous cue rumble with discrete gate ticks, pulse-count charge-band patterns, per-weapon commit signatures, per-node adaptive-trigger ladders and the spider-knife escape wriggle (`feedback.ts`; `dualsense-serializers.ts`; `dualsense-hid.ts`; `engine.ts`; `public/99lc/game-config.json`; `docs/WEB-CLIENT.md` §12A).

### M120. 99LC Unterhaw could disappear or stall instead of extending an immediate Oberhaw
- **Expected:** a double tap fires Oberhaw immediately whenever it is ready; holding that same second press for 1,000 ms adds Unterhaw. Unterhaw cannot start independently or while Oberhaw is cooling down, has a cooldown three times longer than Oberhaw, deals direct damage to the original in-range Oberhaw victim (or the nearest in-range enemy if that victim became unavailable), and uses a real current-aim collider only when Oberhaw missed.
- **Actual before the fix:** `doubleTapHold` could resolve as a standalone Sword action, shared Oberhaw/Unterhaw cooldown state, and queued a delayed follow-up collider. The DualSense route could select the deep hold node before committing Oberhaw; target fallback was direction-only and the pending window could be cleared before a valid follow-up resolved. Depending on input path and timing, Unterhaw was absent or left the action pipeline apparently frozen (`public/99lc/game-config.json` pre-fix `hybrid-sword` controls; `engine.ts` pre-fix `gestureReady`/`performAttack`/`resolveDelayedAttackDirection`).
- **Failure scenario:** with Oberhaw ready, enter double-tap+hold directly or pull through the deep DualSense gate → Oberhaw may not fire at double-tap registration, and releasing after one second may produce no valid Unterhaw or a stale collider/lock.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-21 — the first Sword double-tap node commits Oberhaw on press and arms a continuation-only Unterhaw window; `gestureReady` rejects standalone `doubleTapHold`, while the one-second continuation bypasses only Oberhaw's active/recovery lock. Unterhaw resolves immediately at the gate, owns its ×3 cooldown key, applies collider-free direct damage to the original/nearest priority target, and creates the authored sweep along current aim only after an Oberhaw miss. Focused tests cover original target, vanished-target retarget, missed-Oberhaw collider, cooldown separation and DualSense traversal (`engine.ts` `gestureReady`/`prepareSwordOberhau`/`executePendingUnterhau`/`executeSwordFollowUp`; `config.ts` Sword control seeds; `public/99lc/game-config.json`; `engine.spec.ts`).

### M121. Empire's Endgame legacy imports skipped version-gated state migrations
- **Expected:** an envelope/state from any supported save generation preserves its source schema until the engine applies every intervening config-aware migration.
- **Actual before the fix:** the persistence adapter rewrote legacy state to the current schema before constructing the engine. Version-conditioned normalization therefore treated old data as current and could skip fields or compatibility repairs that did not exist in the source save (`features/empires-endgame/persistence.ts` pre-fix envelope migration).
- **Failure scenario:** import a valid v4 campaign whose Grand Advisor or later runtime fields require a version-gated repair → the import claims to be current before that repair runs, so restore can reject it or retain incomplete state.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-21 — envelope validation now requires matching supported envelope/state versions but preserves the state version; only the returned wrapper is current. The engine remains the sole owner of the final schema advance. The authentic config-v1 artifact and representative custom v1–v17 generations prove direct/one-step config migration and non-mutation. A second compressed fixture contains genuine save checkpoints emitted/restored by the production engines at commits `b1dba5d8` (v1), `d21c8e08` (v2), `f9dde5df` (v3), `79602d95` (v4), `1940a5bf` (v5), `a73a4c2c` (v6), `5e57d597` (v7), `38aab631` (v8), `e7d1b902` (v9), `e775f3cf` (v10), `c9b524a0` (v11), `b64b3c0a` (v12), `e789bc98` (v13), `49f96d9a` (v14) and `473015d4` (v15); every checkpoint independently reaches the same v16 state as direct v1 restore without input mutation (`persistence.ts` `migrateEmpiresSnapshotEnvelope`; `compatibility/authentic-v1-config.fixture.ts`; `compatibility/authentic-v1-save-chain.fixture.ts`; `compatibility/scaffolding.spec.ts`).

### M122. Active minigames could settle against config dependencies absent from their rules identity
- **Expected:** every config value read during settlement is frozen into the active session's rules identity, so changing that value makes restore/settlement fail closed.
- **Actual before the fix:** TD omitted Medical, loyalty, expedition and quest settlement dependencies; Tavern omitted its gold/loyalty dependencies; Alchemy omitted loyalty, quests and the final-day settlement closure; Inventory omitted city, loyalty, quest and full expedition dependencies. Every minigame also omitted the shared result-retention/save policy that can compact its settlement record. A valid plan could therefore restore under a changed config and apply different casualties, rewards, currency, quest effects or end-of-empire processing while retaining the old digest (`features/empires-endgame/engine.ts` pre-fix rules-identity builders).
- **Failure scenario:** save a Tavern visit, change `domesticEconomy.goldResourceId`, then restore and finish → the frozen purchase plan can debit a different resource than the one under which it was created. Equivalent drift existed for expedition TD rewards and Inventory settlement effects.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-21 — all four identities include their settlement closure and the shared result-retention/save policy; the non-rule campaign initialization seed is deliberately excluded. Schema-v15 digest recognizers use each persisted identity's historical config schema and upgrade only authentic old active sessions during save-v16 migration. Legacy IDs/plans and expedition lifecycle links are canonicalized together, so the migrated active session also survives a second current save/reload. Stale-current sessions are rejected before mutation; focused coverage changes each subsystem's dependencies (`engine.ts` `current*RulesIdentity`/`legacyV15*RulesIdentity`/`settlementRulesIdentityConfig`; `__tests__/phase13.spec.ts`).

### M123. Compacted minigame results lost exact-once protection and campaign histories could grow without a hard ceiling
- **Expected:** result detail may compact, but settlement identity remains durable; command/replay work, save size and every long-lived history have enforceable upper bounds.
- **Actual before the fix:** after a result record was evicted, its session ID was no longer recognized and the old plan could be opened and settled again. Ended epidemics, consumed battle-loss IDs and expedition complaint IDs grew indefinitely. Quest trigger digests could prove that *something* was compacted but not whether a repeated trigger had already been consumed. Missing current-save compaction/watermark fields were silently regenerated, God restore sliced overflow without digesting it, and malformed result/expedition histories could reset identity high-water marks. Imported/custom plans had only positive-value checks; count-only result retention could exceed localStorage, a corrupt newest key masked a valid older save, and the pre-normalization byte check rejected genuine legacy history before it could compact (`features/empires-endgame/engine.ts`, `quests.ts`, minigame/config validators and `persistence.ts` pre-fix behavior).
- **Failure scenario:** set result retention to one, settle two sessions, restore the first plan and submit its authentic replay → rewards can be applied twice; a long campaign also accumulates epidemic/loss provenance until localStorage becomes unbounded.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-21 — save schema 16 gives every new session a contiguous canonical sequence and persists a settled-through watermark; legacy IDs retain a bounded compatibility tail. Current snapshots must carry internally consistent result/session/sequence identity plus non-erasable compaction evidence. Results remain idempotent after detail eviction and export/import. Epidemics retain their maximum evicted sequence; canonical battle-loss IDs are accepted only for the matching active TD sequence/city and protected from active-sequence eviction; quest triggers retain per-coordinate watermarks or conservatively sealed kinds; expedition complaints/results retain attempt watermarks; God overflow feeds the same rolling digests as runtime compaction. Shared ceilings now bound ticks, commands, catch-up, logical replay duration, roster/plan/list/board sizes, result/history retention and 4,096 settled sessions. Result detail compacts by count and serialized byte pressure beneath the 512 KiB UTF-8 boundary. Browser autosave and manual export use that same bounded serializer and expose a persistent alert on failure. Startup tests matching storage keys newest-to-oldest through production engine validation, so corrupt, oversized-current or semantically invalid new data cannot mask an older valid campaign; oversized genuine legacy history may still normalize/compact before its first v16 export (`stabilization.ts`; `engine.ts` compaction/normalization helpers; `quests.ts`; `config.ts`; minigame validators; `persistence.ts`; `EmpiresEndgame.vue`; `__tests__/phase13.spec.ts`).

### M124. Alchemy and Inventory global shortcuts hijacked focused controls and destructive abort modals did not suspend input
- **Expected:** minigame keyboard controls act only on the game surface, interactive controls keep native keyboard behavior, and a destructive confirmation is a labelled, focus-trapped modal that pauses simulation and restores focus/state on cancel.
- **Actual before the fix:** window-level shortcuts still handled keys originating in buttons/inputs. The inline abort confirmation neither trapped focus nor stopped the fixed-step loop, so background ticks and commands could continue while the player was deciding (`components/empires-endgame/AlchemyBoard.vue`; `InventoryPacking.vue` pre-fix handlers/dialogs).
- **Failure scenario:** focus a native button and press Space/arrow/Escape, or open abort while a frame backlog exists → both the control and minigame may act, and the simulation advances behind the destructive prompt.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-21 — both boards reject interactive-target and browser-modifier shortcuts, suppress repeated toggle keys, keep Escape available from focused controls, and expose labelled keyboard surfaces without contradictory toggle semantics. They pause/reset their frame clocks while confirmation is open and share a Continue-first `MinigameAbortDialog` with focus trap, Escape/backdrop cancellation, description wiring and focus restoration. Fake-clock, focused-control, visibility and accessibility smoke tests cover the production simulators (`AlchemyBoard.vue`; `InventoryPacking.vue`; `MinigameAbortDialog.vue`; component and engine specs).

### M125. Current-save normalization deleted valid Tavern, Alchemy, expedition and veteran chronicle entries
- **Expected:** every `EmpiresChronicleEntryKind` emitted by current gameplay survives an immediate export/import/engine restore, subject only to configured oldest-first retention.
- **Actual before the fix:** the restore allowlist stopped at the older political/economy kinds and omitted the four later kinds even though live settlement appended them. A current expedition save with three chronicle entries restored with only its battle-loss entry (`features/empires-endgame/engine.ts` pre-fix `normalizeLoyaltyState`).
- **Failure scenario:** complete Inventory → expedition assault, export, and import → the expedition launch/result history silently disappears from the visible chronicle.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-21 — the normalizer accepts `tavern`, `alchemy`, `expedition` and `veteran`; the Phase-13 campaign asserts byte-bounded final save/reload equality after one continuously accumulated engine crosses the full packing/assault chain and retains its six ordered minigame settlements (`engine.ts` `normalizePoliticalState`; `qa.ts` `runEmpiresStabilizationCampaign`; `qa.spec.ts`).

### M126. Battleship setup, combo timing and Final Boarding diverged from the revised designer contract
- **Expected:** setup purchases stay non-negative with Russian priced descriptions; placed ships are draggable and expose their bow, deck modules and declarative effect zones. Combat presents one shared Ballista with cyclic living Mid origins (Close joins only in Boarding), visibly announces a ready Tetracatapult, and puts direct-click summon choices under weapons with spawn flash/`pts combo 1`. Only combo-preserving hits pause eight seconds; misses continue immediately. Destroying an originally enemy CAPTURE-ship credits its capturer with the ship-Space reveal. At Final Boarding only the side without Mid deploys, every mandatory ship is placed before resolution resumes (the bot does all in one step), and a sole living Desiccator wins before deployment while two defer only that auto-win until one dies.
- **Actual before the fix:** upgrade copy was partly English and the client could select purchases after their cost exceeded the visible remainder; placed ships required remove/re-place and exposed neither deck identity nor setup-time effect ranges. Every Ballista module appeared as a separate weapon, reset shots used a fixed two-second wait even after misses, summon deployment had a redundant second button and no arrival cue, and CAPTURE death revealed no Space to the capturer. Boarding converted both fleets' Close line, allowed resolution/bot fire to proceed around partial deployment, and dual Desiccators permanently lost their live passives instead of dynamically deferring only the win rule (`ShipCatalog.cs`; `FleetBuilder.vue`; `PlacementPhase.vue`; `BattleshipService.cs`/`BattleshipGameEngine.cs` pre-fix paths).
- **Failure scenario:** capture an enemy Close, let its original owner destroy it, then trigger Boarding by killing one Mid line while the other remains and the active shooter is a bot → the capturer gets no death reveal, both sides are asked to deploy, and the bot can continue through a human's incomplete placement after a fixed delay; two Desiccators also behave differently from the stated guarantee.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-21 — the catalog/UI now provide Russian priced descriptions and affordability-disabled controls; pointer placement preserves the grabbed deck and derives bow/module/armor badges plus ability-keyed freeze/poison/explosion zones. `MapAvailableWeapons` exposes one shared Ballista and `SelectNextBallistaAnimationSource` cycles operational Mid/Boarding-Close origins; ready catapults produce a full-screen white cue. `SummonBar` enters cell selection directly and board diffs flash/play combo-stack sound for a new summon. The server/client enforce an eight-second hit-only gate and bot misses have no wait. CAPTURE death calls `RevealShip` for the capturer. `TriggerBoarding`, `CompleteActionResolution`/`ResumeBoardingResolution` and the bot pump now form a global all-deployed barrier for only the Mid-less side, while `CheckDesiccatorBoardingWin` applies the dynamic sole-survivor rule without stripping other abilities (`BattleshipGameEngine.cs`; `BattleshipService.cs`; `GameHub.cs`; `FleetBuilder.vue`; `PlacementPhase.vue`; `CombatPhase.vue`; `SummonBar.vue`; `BoardGrid.vue`; `CellComponent.vue`; `battleship.ts`).

### M127. Гарем но джутсу paid for canceled fights instead of provoked enemy skips
- **Intended:** an active Гарем но джутсу gives the original Наруто exactly +1 regular point for each enemy whose reaching attack queue is canceled and converted into a Skip; no reaching enemy means no points. Each such enemy Skip also emits «Техника соблазнения!» to Naruto.
- **Actual before the fix:** `ResolveHaremQueues` summed every valid target in each reaching attacker's queue, while the dynamic BFG/Railgun path paid `validTargets.Count`. One enemy converted into one Skip could therefore pay several points merely because its queue contained unrelated fights, making Naruto appear to earn points from players who never attacked him; neither path emitted the requested phrase (`Naruto.cs:212-232,243-268`).
- **Failure scenario:** an enemy has three valid queued fights and one target is the active Harem → the enemy becomes one Skip, but Naruto receives +3 regular points and no «Техника соблазнения!» line instead of +1 and one line.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-21 — both finalized-queue and dynamic cancellation paths now call the same one-enemy reward helper: +1 regular point, +1 Harem achievement progress and one «Техника соблазнения!» phrase per newly converted attacker. `SkipPlayersThisRound` likewise records one provoked Skip rather than the canceled queue length (`Naruto.RewardHaremSkip`; `ResolveHaremQueues`; `TryCancelHaremFights`).
- **Superseded by designer rework, 2026-07-27:** Harem no longer clears an attacker's queue or pays +1 regular. It turns Naruto's own action into Skip, so only each incoming Harem fight is recorded as skipped and every other queued fight remains. Each ordinary attacker instead transfers `ceil(20% × lifetime ability income)`, minimum 1 bonus point, once per activation; the related achievement now counts three successful donations (`Naruto.ResolveHaremQueues`/`HaremSkipAppliesTo`/`RewardHaremDonation`; `InGameStatus.GetLifetimeAbilityPoints`; `AchievementClass` `c_naruto_harem`).
- **Re-verified:** 2026-07-30 — `GetLifetimeAbilityPoints` still combines every settled round's positive non-natural bonus and multiplier-applied regular ledger with the current round's projected eligible entries; natural victory points remain excluded. An executable smoke case with 29 lifetime ability points produced `ceil(29 × 20%) = 6` and an exact −6/+6 transfer, confirming the report was not reproducible in the current working tree and required no further gameplay change.

### M128. The Phase-13 “integrated campaign” gate did not accumulate one campaign
- **Expected:** one deterministic campaign crosses every live subsystem in order, then exports/reloads that same accumulated state; a repeated seed proves the complete final snapshot, digest and result are reproducible.
- **Actual before the fix:** the runner created separate fixture engines for most subsystems and summed their actions into one report. The alleged final engine therefore did not contain the prior Tavern, Alchemy, TD, economy, quest, epidemic or loyalty settlements, while the repeat assertion compared only a reduced result object. The gate could pass even when cross-system state was lost between steps (`features/empires-endgame/qa.ts` pre-fix `runEmpiresStabilizationCampaign`; `qa.spec.ts`).
- **Failure scenario:** break retention of an early Tavern or Alchemy result during the later expedition save/reload → each isolated fixture still passes and the final “campaign” cannot observe the loss.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-21 — every alpha/beta/gamma run now mutates one engine, records exactly 13 ordered checkpoints and finishes with one ordered six-result log (Tavern, Alchemy, defense TD, assault TD, Inventory, expedition TD). Explicit/staged and autoplay accounting satisfies `steps = autoplaySteps + 35`; repeated beta compares the exact final snapshot, digest and result. Maria's unavailable encounter prerequisite is staged, but the campaign then performs the live 3→7→Ace observer with public card actions and retains the Пиковая Дама spawn/history. Measured totals are 46/48/48 actions and 80,682/82,427/84,851 UTF-8 bytes (`qa.ts` `runEmpiresStabilizationCampaign`; `qa.spec.ts`).

### M129. Inventory packing could consume the last empire day without ending the phase
- **Expected:** when expedition preparation spends the final available empire day, settling or aborting its Inventory session resumes the ordinary end-of-empire pipeline exactly once, just like Alchemy and other day-consuming actions.
- **Actual before the fix:** `beginExpeditionPacking` deducted preparation days before entering Inventory, but `settleInventoryOutcome` returned to the empire phase without calling the final-day settlement. The player could remain in an impossible empire phase with zero days and continue through paths that assumed time remained (`features/empires-endgame/engine.ts` pre-fix `settleInventoryOutcome`).
- **Failure scenario:** start packing with exactly the expedition's preparation-day cost, then complete or abort → Inventory closes, but phase remains `empire` at zero days instead of processing famine/event/card transition.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-21 — after trusted Inventory effects, result identity and quest triggers settle, the handler runs `finishEmpireInternal` when it returned to empire with no days and no mandatory dialogue blocker. The durable settlement watermark is therefore recorded before ordinary next-con processing may schedule a due TD wave. The active-session rules identity includes the full settlement closure, and final-day regressions assert both phase advance and this sequence order (`engine.ts` `settleInventoryOutcome`/`finishEmpireAfterDayConsumingMinigame`; `__tests__/phase13.spec.ts`).

### M130. Dopa's double Взгляд в будущее reward ignored that one selected target blocked
- **Expected:** Взгляд в будущее can reward Dopa only after both distinct Макро attacks become real fights and one chosen target has the other as its next target. A Block or unbroken Skip on either selected enemy prevents that attack from resolving, so neither the ordinary +2 nor Фарм's doubled +4 may proc.
- **Actual before the fix:** the end-of-round check read only Dopa's two submitted target IDs and those targets' submitted queues. It never verified that both Dopa attacks passed the Block/Skip no-fight gates. With one target blocking, the remaining relationship could therefore grant the full reward—and Фарм made the false reward look doubled—even though Dopa had only one real fight (`CharacterPassives.cs` pre-fix Взгляд в будущее case).
- **Failure scenario:** Dopa selects A and B; A targets B while B Blocks. Dopa resolves only the fight against A, but the submitted A→B edge still produced +2 regular points, or +4 under Фарм, plus 50 Skill.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-21 — Макро now counts submitted/processed actions separately from fights that reach the after-fight hook; Взгляд requires `FightsResolved >= 2` before testing the cross-target relationship, and both counters reset together next round. Duplicate second targets are also rejected without erasing the first choice. The private/shadow fight marker is finalized and cleared in block, skip and resolved paths so a canceled second action cannot leak its hidden flag into another fight (`CP:1633-1640,2376-2378,4834-4865,6306-6309`; `DoomsdayMachine.MarkHiddenFight`; `GameReactions.HandleAttack`).
- **Reverted:** 2026-07-22 — designer redesign made this the intended behavior's opposite: a selected target's Block/Skip must **not** deny the proc (Vision now judges by aim, not by fights resolving), so the `FightsResolved >= 2` gate was removed. `FightsResolved` is now unused by Vision (increment left in place, harmless); the duplicate-target rejection and shadow-flag finalization from this fix are retained. See Dopa in `docs/CHARACTERS.md`.
- **Rework follow-up:** 2026-08-04 — aim remains authoritative, but multiplicity no longer collapses through `Contains`: every matching finalized queue entry now grants its own points, +50 Skill and achievement proc. Twelve repeated fights between the predicted pair therefore pay twelve times (`CharacterPassives.HandleEndOfRound`).

### M131. 99LC character movement could lose the whole input vector against room walls
- **Expected:** contact with an obstacle or arena edge removes only the blocked component; the character keeps moving along the free wall tangent at the authored movement speed instead of sticking or becoming slower.
- **Actual before the fix:** `moveCircle` attempted X and Y independently and reverted either endpoint that overlapped an obstacle. At wall/corner contacts, both endpoint probes could fail in the same frame, discarding the complete movement delta; the axis-sized fallback also could not preserve the intended travel length (`features/last-chances/engine.ts` pre-fix `moveCircle`).
- **Failure scenario:** hold a diagonal movement input into the side or corner of a solid obstacle → the character can stop until the player first steers away from the wall instead of sliding along it.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-21 — circle movement now advances in short continuous steps, binary-searches each contact point, and spends the unresolved distance on the best free X/Y tangent. Tangential travel retains the original step length, so player, enemy, dash and forced-movement callers receive full-speed wall sliding without tunneling through authored obstacles (`engine.ts` `circleCanOccupy`/`maximumSafeMovementFraction`/`moveCircle`; focused engine regression). **M164 superseded this fix's head-on carve-out for player walking only:** a step with no tangent now slides toward the blocking wall's nearest end instead of stopping dead centre. Dashes, knockback and enemy movement keep the behavior described here.

### M132. Выгодная сделка did not steal exactly half of the target's actual tactic earnings
- **Expected:** each target's `SellerTacticBonusEarned` ledger sums the actual bonus amount credited by every `Впарить говна` award. When the Seller wins an attack against that target on round 10, both the debit and paired credit use exactly half of that complete persistent ledger, including a fractional 0.5 point for odd totals.
- **Actual before the fix:** the earning hook added a hardcoded one ledger unit after every base +1 award, even when Король Сервера transformed the actual credit to +2. Mark expiry correctly kept the ledger, but collection then used `Math.Ceiling(debt / 2)`, so odd recorded debts were also rounded upward (`CharacterPassives.cs` pre-fix `Впарить говна` global after-fight and `Выгодная сделка` attack-after-fight cases).
- **Failure scenarios:** an active Король Сервера earns +2 from one tactic win but enters round 10 with only 1 debt recorded; an ordinary target earns one +1 tactic bonus and later loses to the Seller's round-10 attack, which steals 1 instead of the exact 0.5 half.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-21 — the earning hook snapshots round bonus earnings around `AddBonusPoints` and adds the actual credited delta to the persistent ledger; collection transfers `debt / 2` directly as a decimal. D12's intended zero-floor cushion still credits the full nominal half if the victim cannot pay it all (`CP:1903-1916,2606-2617,6253-6279`; Король interaction in `INTERACTION-MATRIX.md` §4).

### M133. Character-specific leaderboard markers leaked to admins, and Джон Сноу's 🏰/🐺 were fully public
- **Expected:** every character-specific leaderboard prefix marker is visible only to its owning character — not to opponents, spectators, or admins. Admins keep enemy stat/identity inspection through `AdminPlayerType`, but not the tactical emoji markers. The only public prefix marker is the round-10 🚫 (Тигр always; Dopa after Permaban). This reverses M35's «(or an admin)» carve-out and m52's public-🏰 decision by designer request.
- **Actual before the fix:** `GameUpdateMess.CustomLeaderBoardBeforeNumber` still granted every real admin (`player1.PlayerType == 2`) a cross-character peek at Goblin ⛏️🏛️🛡️, Salldorum 🥤, sealed-Мадара 🚫 and DooM 🔥🎯. Джон Сноу's Черный Замок 🏰 (place-4 cell) and the two 🐺 weakest-player marks were emitted with **no viewer gate at all**, so every opponent and spectator saw them and could infer Jon's presence in the roster and which players were marked.
- **Failure scenario:** any player opens a game containing Джон Сноу → the place-4 🏰 and two 🐺 marks appear on their own leaderboard even though they are not Jon; separately, a `PlayerType == 2` admin sees DooM's fire nests and every other tactical marker without owning those characters — both leak the hidden-roster/tactical information M35 protects.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-21 — dropped the `|| player1.PlayerType == 2` admin clause from the Goblin, Salldorum, sealed-Мадара and DooM (nests + counter-attack) markers, and gated the 🏰/🐺 emitters on `player1.GetPlayerId() == jonSnow.GetPlayerId()`. Round-10 🚫 (`Tigr.IsRoundTenBanned`, covering Тигр + Dopa-after-Permaban) stays public; the viewer-passive-keyed suffix builder and the `AdminPlayerType` stat/identity suffix are unchanged (`GameUpdateMess.cs` `CustomLeaderBoardBeforeNumber`). See M35 (admin carve-out removed) and m52 (public 🏰 reverted to owner-only).

### M134. 99LC's Unterhau could only ever fire in an already-cleared room

- **Expected:** holding the second Oberhau press for the authored `unterhauHoldMs` (1,000 ms) adds Unterhau, in combat exactly as in a cleared room.
- **Actual before the fix:** `prepareSwordOberhau` armed the follow-up with `unterhauDueAtMs = this.elapsedMs + unterhauHoldMs`, where `elapsedMs` is the *previous* frame's accumulated time, while the hold gate in `updateImmediateSwordInputs` compared `input.heldMs` measured from the real `performance.now()` at press — strictly later than that frame stamp. `update()` then disarmed the follow-up as soon as `elapsedMs >= unterhauDueAtMs`, so the expiry clock always won by up to a frame and `executePendingUnterhau` bailed at its `unterhauDueAtMs <= 0` guard. `updateClearedRoom` had no expiry block at all, which is precisely why the move appeared to work only once every enemy was dead. The `!delayedAttacks.some(… 'swordFollowUp')` escape could never be true either, because `queueSwordUnterhau` is always called with `delayMs = 0` and runs inline (`features/last-chances/engine.ts` pre-fix `update`/`prepareSwordOberhau`/`executePendingUnterhau`). Existing coverage missed it because the sword tests mutate `access.elapsedMs` by hand and never call `update()`.
- **Failure scenario:** double-tap and hold the Mercenary Sword with any enemy alive → Oberhau lands, the hold passes 1,000 ms, and nothing happens; kill the room first and the identical input resolves every time.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-24 — the primed follow-up now carries its own `unterhauExpiresAtMs = unterhauDueAtMs + unterhauWindowMs` (900 ms, authored), which is what the expiry tests; the due stamp is left purely as the arming/gate value. Both `update` and `updateClearedRoom` run the same `expirePendingUnterhau`, and the disarm paths share `disarmPendingUnterhau`. Regression coverage steps the real loop across the gate instead of mutating `elapsedMs` (`engine.ts` `expirePendingUnterhau`/`disarmPendingUnterhau`/`prepareSwordOberhau`; `public/99lc/game-config.json` `hybrid-sword.tuning.unterhauWindowMs`; `engine.spec.ts`).

### M135. 99LC's Unterhau dealt collider-free damage to a live target, gated on the wrong range, and could dereference a dead one

- **Expected:** the follow-up swings for real in every case — at the Oberhau victim, at a nearest replacement, or along the aim — with the authored sweep collider each time.
- **Actual before the fix:** with a live automatic target `executeSwordFollowUp` skipped `executeAttack` entirely and applied `damageEnemy` directly after only a trace and a flash, so the swing had no active area, could not touch anything else, and read on screen as "nothing happened"; only the no-target branch spawned the real sweep. It also gated reach on `weapon.attacks.doubleTap.range` (138) rather than the Unterhau's own 146. Its target lookup did not filter by state, and `originalTarget?.state !== 'dead'` is `true` when the enemy is `undefined`, so the very next expression dereferenced `originalTarget.position` (`features/last-chances/engine.ts` pre-fix `executeSwordFollowUp`).
- **Failure scenario:** land Oberhau, hold into Unterhau while the victim lives → damage is applied with no visible or collidable swing; separately, a `targetEnemyId` whose enemy is no longer in `this.enemies` throws a `TypeError` mid-frame.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-24 — the follow-up resolves its direction (original target while alive and in reach → nearest living enemy in reach → current aim) and then always calls `executeAttack`, so `performMelee`/`startActiveArea` produce the authored collider, trace and effect in all three cases. Reach now uses the Unterhau's own `range`, and the lookup uses the existing `find(enemy => enemy.id === id && enemy.state !== 'dead')` idiom (`engine.ts` `executeSwordFollowUp`; `engine.spec.ts`).

### M136. 99LC's Mercenary Sword punished patient taps and answered fatigue by disabling itself

- **Expected:** fatigue represents rushing the rhythm, and a tired arm makes swinging expensive rather than impossible.
- **Actual before the fix:** `applySwordRhythm` counted a miss for `interval < perfectStartMs || interval > perfectEndMs`, so waiting longer than the 100 ms perfect window was penalised exactly as hard as rushing it, and `gestureReady` returned `false` for `swordRhythm` + `tap` while `fatigueMs > 0`, which reads in play as the weapon breaking (`features/last-chances/engine.ts` pre-fix `applySwordRhythm`/`gestureReady`). Separately, `doubleTapHold` stayed behind the elite combo quest, so the Sword's signature follow-up was unreachable in ordinary play even once the timing bugs above were fixed.
- **Failure scenario:** tap the Sword deliberately slowly → fatigue arrives anyway and the basic attack stops responding for 2,000 ms.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-24 — a miss is now only `interval < perfectStartMs`; a slow tap still shows the `late` cue and clears the consecutive chain like any clean hit. The tap block is gone: fatigue instead multiplies that weapon's stamina cost by `fatigueStaminaMultiplier` (10). `doubleTapHold` is exempt from the move-quest lock for `swordRhythm` weapons only, so every other weapon still earns it from the elite combo quest (`engine.ts` `applySwordRhythm`/`gestureReady`/`staminaCostFor`; `public/99lc/game-config.json` `hybrid-sword.tuning.fatigueStaminaMultiplier`; `engine.spec.ts`).

### M137. Clash ability journals could redirect self effects and spawns across battle sides

- **Expected:** the immutable ability definition determines which side an activation may affect. A self/ally morale action cannot be redirected to the enemy, and a spawned unit can enter only a cell on its source side so `ClashCellState.side` and `ClashUnitState.side` remain consistent.
- **Actual before the fix:** the morale branch trusted `command.targetSide` even for `target: 'self'`. The spawn branch likewise looked up any commanded side but always stamped the new unit with `source.side`. A forged replay journal could buff/debuff the wrong side or put an attacker-owned unit inside a defender-owned cell (`features/empires-endgame/clash/engine.ts` pre-fix `applyAbilityEffect`).
- **Failure scenario:** submit `qa-morale` from an attacker with `targetSide: 'defender'`, or submit a spawn into an empty defender cell; both commands passed the ordinary activation checks and mutated the opposing side.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-25 — morale derives its side from the authored target (`self`/ally → source, enemy → opponent) and rejects a contradictory command side. Spawn rejects every cell outside the source side before creating state. Focused regressions prove both forged journals leave morale, cells, units and the accepted command log unchanged (`features/empires-endgame/clash/engine.ts` `applyAbilityEffect`; `clash/engine.spec.ts`).

### M138. Authentic pre-Phase-14 active minigames failed the advertised save migration

- **Expected:** a supported v17/v18 bundled save with an authentic active TD, Tavern, Alchemy, Inventory or Clash rules identity migrates to the exact known current bundled successor. The bridge must not rebind that identity to modified or custom current rules.
- **Actual before the fix:** `legacyV17RulesIdentity` and `legacyV18RulesIdentity` attempted to reconstruct historical rules by projecting the current config backwards. Later edits to shared settlement/config dependencies changed those projections, so their digests did not match the identities actually emitted by the historical bundle. The compatibility test duplicated the same projection and therefore could not expose the failure (`features/empires-endgame/engine.ts` pre-fix `legacyV17ConfigProjection`/`legacyV18ConfigProjection`; `phase14.spec.ts` pre-fix compatibility fixture).
- **Failure scenario:** restore a real pre-Phase-14 save with an active v17 TD session, or a Phase-14 v18 Clash session, under the current bundle; normalization rejects it with “active minigame rules identity does not match” even though its schema is supported.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-25 — immutable, independently sourced v17/v18 bundled digests are accepted only as pinned pairs whose destination also matches the known current bundled digest. Authentic TD, Tavern, Alchemy, Inventory and Clash regressions now cover input immutability and successful upgrade; a modified current ruleset remains rejected (`features/empires-endgame/engine.ts` `AUTHENTIC_BUNDLED_LEGACY_RULES_DIGESTS`/`isAuthenticBundledLegacyRulesUpgrade`; `compatibility/authentic-v17-v18-rules.fixture.ts`; `phase14.spec.ts`).

### M139. Clash import limits were per-array and allowed an unbounded aggregate rules graph

- **Expected:** P13's 256-item immutable-plan ceiling bounds the total Clash definition graph at both config import and active-plan restore boundaries; the separate 2,048-entry roster-instance ceiling continues to bound campaign actors.
- **Actual before the fix:** config and plan validation capped several top-level arrays and each unit's passive/ability arrays independently. A plan with 256 unit definitions could therefore carry 256 passives plus 256 abilities on every unit while still satisfying every individual 256-item check, multiplying validation and replay work far beyond the documented import budget (`features/empires-endgame/clash/engine.ts` pre-fix `validateClashConfig`/`validateClashPlan`).
- **Failure scenario:** import a syntactically valid Clash plan containing 256 units with hundreds of uniquely named nested components each; restore traverses the entire expanded graph instead of rejecting it at the bounded boundary.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-25 — config validation now aggregates fields, statuses, terrain, regions, roster definitions, routes, deferred features, terrain cells and nested passives/abilities. Plan validation aggregates field/region carriers, unit/status/terrain definitions, terrain cells and every nested passive/ability, with the existing actor-roster limit kept separate. Both boundaries reject totals above 256, and focused fixtures prove nested content can no longer multiply the ceiling (`features/empires-endgame/clash/engine.ts` `clashConfigComponentCount`/`validateClashPlan`; `clash/engine.spec.ts`).

### M140. Battleship's Alliance roster, maneuver privacy and summon-death projection were absent

- **Expected:** the 2026-07-25 Battleship specification enables a 50-coin Alliance with faction-specific ships and a diagonal flagship; every Mid deck owns a Ballista and its VFX uses Mid or actually deployed Boarding-Close coordinates; both maneuver mechanics preserve Space and stay hidden from the opponent until death; a damaged manual mover blocks all other actions until a legal board-cell choice; freeze reveals the summon death cell, and every dead summon retains its own typed map marker.
- **Actual before the fix:** Alliance was disabled and hard-forced back to Empire/40 coins; fleet definitions had no faction filter or diagonal geometry. Manual movement used direction buttons, ignored allied Space and leaked the live destination through already revealed fog cells; Light Wood dodge reused scratched/miss presentation. Ballista animation mixed every Close into Boarding and inferred its source from the ship's stale home coordinate. Freeze cleared a summon without revealing its cell, cells retained only typed trails, and bow/module badges were placement-only (`FleetValidator`; `BattleshipService.SelectArmy`/`SelectNextBallistaAnimationSource`/`MapFogBoard`; `BattleshipGameEngine.TryMoveShip`/`ManualMoveShip`/`FreezeSummon`; pre-fix Battleship Vue phase/grid components).
- **Failure scenario:** choose Alliance (button is disabled), or move a revealed Maneuvering Double/Light Wood Triple and watch the opponent board expose the destination immediately; a frozen Scout disappears without resolving the cell beneath it, and two summons dying on one cell leave no individual death record.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-25 — faction-filtered 40/50 budgets now provide mutually exclusive Tetranavis/Знаменитый диагональный корабль defaults plus Alliance-only Горючая баржа, Маневрирующая двойка, Пираты and Знаменитый Врезающийся корабль. Canonical geometry drives diagonal placement/Space/hits; every Mid deck gets a Ballista. Server-authored source board/cell drives Mid versus deployed-Close VFX. Legal maneuver endpoints enforce Space, form a mandatory action lock and remain fog-private until `RevealShip`; the ramming variant destroys overlapping allied deck modules/weapons. Light Wood uses an explicit pink dodge state. Freeze reveals its death cell, ordered typed death records preserve one icon per summon, and combat keeps owner bow/module/armor badges (`ShipCatalog`; `FleetValidator`; `BattleshipGameEngine`; `BattleshipService`; `battleship-geometry.ts`; `FleetBuilder.vue`; `CombatPhase.vue`; `BoardGrid.vue`; `CellComponent.vue`).

### M141. Battleship reset windows stalled bot/no-summon barrages and summon-entry deaths skipped Boarding

- **Expected:** the 8-second reset exists only when a defending human can legally deploy a summon; every other retained-shot pause is 2 seconds, including a human firing at a bot.
- **Superseded 2026-07-26 (designer decision, not a regression):** the `CanDeployAnySummon` half of that condition was removed. The 8-second window now applies whenever the board that lost the deck belongs to a human, whether the shooter is a human or a bot; only a bot-owned defending board gets 2 seconds. The defect this finding fixed remains fixed — a human firing at a bot still never waits more than 2 seconds. The accepted cost is that a human whose summons are exhausted still waits 8 seconds per reset hit; the designer chose this so the window can never be shortened by a wrong prediction about the defender's options. See BALANCE-CONSTANTS.md "Battleship combo-hit response window" and BattleshipGDD.md §3.1. Both players see the active reload, a hit projectile travels slowly while that reload is already running, and the defender may deploy during the enemy window. Destroyed decks, fully sunk ships and far rows must remain visually distinct. Any immediate summon spawn/re-entry collision that removes the last Mid must enter the same global Boarding transition, converting only the Mid-less side (or both sides if both lines disappear in that resolution).
- **Superseded again 2026-07-27 (designer decision):** the original response-aware condition is restored. Eight seconds now require `CanDeployAnySummon` for a human defender at the post-resolution state boundary; a human with no currently legal summon placement gets the same two-second retained-shot pause as every bot-owned board. The server and client contracts in the linked documents are authoritative.
- **Actual before the fix:** every combo-preserving hit hard-coded an 8-second server/bot delay even if the defender was a bot, had not unlocked a summon, was on summon cooldown, had exhausted its cap or had no legal entry. The Vue store rendered only the caller's delay, started its local fixed-eight-second assumption after dispatching VFX, used normal projectile speed for hits, and did not expose server-authoritative response availability. Sunk cells reused the destroyed-deck treatment and rows 9–10 were not differentiated. Most importantly, `DeploySummon`/`DeployPendingSummon` registered immediate fire/Freeze/collision outside `CompleteActionResolution` and never repeated the Boarding/win checks, so a deployment that killed the last Mid left the game in Combat; an automatic Boarding-entry Pirate could also remove the other last Mid after the eligible-side set had already been captured.
- **Failure scenario:** a human hits a bot and waits the full eight seconds despite there being no possible response; or a Pirate/Ram enters on the last Mid deck, destroys it immediately, and no mandatory Boarding deployment appears.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-25 — `ApplyComboShotDelay` selects 8 seconds only from `CanDeployAnySummon` for a human defender and otherwise selects 2; `ShotResult.ShotDelayMs` plus both players' delay DTOs drive one public reload bar, and the bot pump waits that exact interval lock-free. Summon controls use caller-only server availability outside the caller's turn. Hit projectiles take 3.2 seconds and the delay starts before their VFX. Fully sunk ships use a dark crossed treatment and rows 9–10 are dimmed. Every spawn/re-entry calls `ResolveImmediateEffectTransitions`, while `TriggerBoarding` re-evaluates Mid-less players after its automatic Pirate entries (`BattleshipService` `CanDeployAnySummon`/`ApplyComboShotDelay`/`ResolveImmediateEffectTransitions`; `BattleshipGameEngine.QueueBoardingShipsForMidlessPlayers`; `GameHub.SendBattleshipShotEvent`/`RunBattleshipBotPump`; `battleship.ts`; `CombatPhase.vue`; `ProjectileLayer.vue`; `BoardGrid.vue`; `CellComponent.vue`).

### M142. 99LC player movement skips the authored acceleration and braking model

- **Expected:** the prototype movement task explicitly requires separate acceleration and braking parameters, externally editable with the other gameplay numbers. DeepList specifies reaching full walking speed in roughly six 60-Hz frames and stopping in roughly three, while a new direction pressed during braking must take control immediately (`DiscordExports/99LC/99LC - development - mechanics [1125045645552652359].json`, messages `1125495728509296650`, `1125495856645296188`, `1125497866534801549`, `1125503924099633282`, `1126175360166412378`).
- **Actual before the fix:** every ordinary movement frame multiplied the normalized input directly by `moveSpeed`; releasing an input changed that vector straight to zero. `player` exposed only maximum speed, so neither ramp could be represented in JSON or tuned in the Builder (pre-fix movement frame in `Web/VueClient/src/features/last-chances/engine.ts`; the `player` block of `Web/VueClient/public/99lc/game-config.json`; player fields in `Web/VueClient/src/components/last-chances/BuilderDrawer.vue`).
- **Failure scenario:** press or release a movement direction at 60 Hz → the character jumps between zero and full speed in one frame, so the requested six-frame start and three-frame stop never occur; no configuration-only change can test a different feel.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-25 — `player.accelerationMs` and `player.decelerationMs` are required, validated schema fields, shipped at 100/50 ms and exposed in both Builder languages. Walking velocity now ramps independently toward full speed or rest, while a new input direction takes control immediately and root/recovery hard-stop it. Migration backfills authentic older overrides, and fake-frame regressions prove both ramps and the no-turn-lag rule (`types.ts` `LastChancesConfig.player`; `config.ts` `backfillPlayerMovementFields`; `engine.ts` `updatePlayer`; `BuilderDrawer.vue`).

### M143. Salldorum's «Переписать историю» did not convert every historical loss into a full win

- **Expected:** through turn 9 inclusive, choosing a completed round converts every separate Salldorum loss on that round into a replacement win. Salldorum receives the victory point at his actual old multiplier, the historical underdog Moral and any Сильный-class win Skill; every actual recipient of the original positive victory point loses its own multiplied point. No control is added to the character widget.
- **Actual before the fix:** both server and Chronicle UI closed the active as round 8 began, making turn 7 the last chance. The ledger collapsed all same-enemy losses into one row, awarded no Moral or win-only class Skill, and credited Salldorum once per recorded recipient rather than once per converted loss. Consequently a repeated loss paid only once, while one point duplicated across several Евреи could overpay Salldorum; a suppressed original winner point also suppressed his otherwise valid replacement point (`Salldorum.cs` pre-fix `WinPointRecipients`/`RewriteHistory`; `Game.vue` pre-fix `rewriteHistoryRounds`).
- **Failure scenario:** Salldorum loses two resolved fights to one enemy on round 5 while standing below them and using the Сильный class, then rewrites that round → the old resolver grants at most one ×2 point, zero Moral and zero class Skill instead of two separately reconstructed wins. With two Евреи stealing one original win, it instead credits two replacement points for a single loss.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-25 — every resolved Salldorum loss now snapshots his hypothetical old point multiplier/Moral/Сильный win Skill plus each actual point recipient and that recipient's multiplier. Rewrite iterates every loss, credits one replacement win, debits every recipient, preserves the Сайтама-style nominal credit across recipient score floors and rejects unknown_bug debits. Server, both bot policies and the existing Chronicle action remain live through turn 9; the last-chance treatment moved from round 7 to 9, with no PlayerCard/widget action added (`Salldorum.RecordHistoricalLoss`/`RewriteHistory`; `DoomsdayMachine` win branches; `BotsBehavior`; `Game.vue` `rewriteHistoryRounds`/`rewriteHistoryLastChance`; v5.1.8).

### M144. 99LC validation ignores guaranteed enemies when proving room safety

- **Expected:** an externally authored run definition must be rejected before play when any enemy eligible for a room cannot fit its spawn layout, or when a boss eligible for that room lacks its required altar/arena infrastructure. This is part of the prototype requirement that every spawn and gameplay number remain externally editable without producing an invalid run (prototype requirements document `1j9mTfhZJM2pxANJa9GQllPkxfgOywadYfYHykz8U8Mg`; `docs/WEB-CLIENT.md` §12A).
- **Actual before the fix:** per-room spawn radii considered only weighted `enemyPool` entries; `guaranteedEnemyIds` affected a global fallback but not any room-specific radius. Boss infrastructure checks considered only fixed encounters and boss-tier labels, not enemies supplied by an ordinary tier's weighted or guaranteed lists (`features/last-chances/config.ts` pre-fix `eligibleEnemySpawnRadii`/`validateContentReferences`).
- **Failure scenario:** increase guaranteed Colossus radius from 58 to 140 → validation succeeds, but generation can place it at `(1146, 542)` in the 1248×702 `wrong-shadow-event`, beyond the legal maximum x=1108. Or add `cockroach-mother` to tier 1 `guaranteedEnemyIds` → validation succeeds and can generate her in a room with no altar and no four linked boss holes.
- **Fixed:** 2026-07-25 — schema-v8+ geometry and content validation now maps each room to weighted, guaranteed and fixed-encounter enemies, checks the largest eligible radius and boss-specific infrastructure, and rejects the 140-radius Colossus and ordinary-tier Mother fixtures. Guaranteed enemies reserve slots inside `enemyCount`; validation also handles a guarantee set larger than that target instead of allowing silent runtime truncation. The shipped layouts were moved only where the stricter proof exposed real boundary/obstacle violations, and genuine older definitions receive those canonical repairs during migration (`config.ts` `eligibleEnemySpawnRadii`/`validateSpawnGeometry`/`validateContentReferences`; `plan.ts` `makeEnemyPlan`; `config.spec.ts`).

### M145. 99LC randomizes away its authored first encounter and replays the prologue on regeneration

- **Expected:** the story opens in the false apartment, reveals the missing knives and then presents Нож-паук as the first enemy before procedural route choice begins. Manually replacing a generation restarts that run layout; it does not replay the entire opening story (project GDD `1mpT03MyXtV1tW-j2uFbG7KvdnYqJKnBOc2GyTBw5hcE`; `DiscordExports/99LC/99LC - Visual - 3d-graphics [1125046053138337902].json`, message `1153340186147692616`).
